[EVENT: TUR31463]
Date: 2023-10-01
Country: Turkey | Location: Cankaya
Type: Battles > Armed clash | Disorder: Political violence
Actor1: PKK: Kurdistan Workers Party [Rebel group]
Actor2: Police Forces of Turkey (2016-) [State forces]
Fatalities: 2
Sources: ANF News; Beyaz Gazete; Bianet; Rudaw [National-Regional]

DESCRIPTION:
On 1 October 2023, 2 PKK militants attacked the ministry of interior building on Ataturk Boulevard in Cankaya town, Ankara. The militants arrived by a car in front of the building's gated entrance, opened fire at the police officers guarding the gate. One of the militants approached the gate while firing his weapon and set off the bomb vest he was carrying, causing a large explosion. The other militant was killed during the shootout before he could detonate his bomb vest. 2 police officers at the gate were injured due to the detonation, and 2 militants that carried out the attack died.

---

[EVENT: TUR31434]
Date: 2023-10-04
Country: Turkey | Location: Ankara
Type: Strategic developments > Arrests | Disorder: Strategic developments
Actor1: Military Forces of Turkey (2016-) Gendarmerie [State forces]
Actor2: Civilians (Turkey) [Civilians]
Fatalities: 0
Sources: ANF News; Beyaz Gazete; Bianet; Mezopotamya Agency [National-International]

DESCRIPTION:
Around 4 October 2023 (between 2 - 6 October), Turkish police and gendarmerie forces arrested around 250 people who are suspected to be affiliated with PKK, during raids conducted in Izmir, Eskisehir, Kilis, Kocaeli, Batman, Hakkari, Mardin, Diyarbakir, Agri, Bitlis and Sirnak provinces. HDP executives and journalists were among the detainees. Turkey has launched nation wide counterterrorism operations in retaliation for a suicide bombing in Ankara by the outlawed PKK over the weekend (Coded to Ankara).

---

Security measures: On 20 October 2023, Tunceli Governor's office issued a ban on all forms of marches, protests, press conferences, sit-ins and other political gatherings in the province for 5 days. Similar measures have been imposed in the province (and several other south-eastern provinces) for some years to maintain public order, presumably against political events that can be organized by the Kurdish movement.
